{"forms":[{"form":"the Gibbard-Satterthwaite theorem","tags":["canonical"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"name","senses":[{"glosses":["A theorem dealing with deterministic ordinal electoral systems that choose a single winner, stating that for every voting rule, one of the following three things must hold: (i) the rule is dictatorial, i.e. there exists a distinguished voter who can choose the winner; or (ii) the rule limits the possible outcomes to two alternatives only; or (iii) the rule is susceptible to tactical voting: in certain conditions some voter's sincere ballot may not defend their opinion best."]}],"upstream_line":7765173,"upstream_offset":17817088786,"upstream_record_sha256":"5bf8310b09ed2ab6a7f88f09fc47156fa2c0201e9ef753142a9b768f3e61b0d6","word":"Gibbard-Satterthwaite theorem"}
